\displaystyle\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{\ref{snake:Hori}}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathop{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}}_{\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}}\forall{\mathit{r}^{*}}.\big[(\neg\{\texttt{ru}\}\sqcap\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{t}})\to\left((\exists{\mathit{r}}.\top)\sqcap\bigsqcup_{\mathrm{t}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{T}\ \text{satisfying cond. (i)--(iii) of }\mathrm{\ref{snake:Hori}}}\forall{\mathit{r}}.\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{t}^{\prime}}\right)\!\big].

We can now define
\mathrm{C}_{\includegraphics[scale]{graphics/icons8-snake-30.png}}{D}
as the conjunction of all the concept definitions presented above.
It follows immediately from the semantics of
\mathcal{ALCO}_{\textsf{reg}}^{\mathit{r}\#\mathit{s}\#}
that the presented concept definition is consistent if and only there exists an element starting a pseudosnake.

Note that the property that pseudosnakes are missing in order to be proper snakes, is the ability to measure.
We tackle this issue by introducing a gadget called a “yardstick”.

{defi}

Let
\mathrm{T}
be a finite non-empty set, let
\mathsf{N}_{\text{\faIcon{ruler}}}^{\mathrm{T}}\coloneqq\{\texttt{st},\texttt{md},\texttt{md}_{\mathrm{t}},\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}\mid\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}\}
be composed of (pairwise different) individual names.
A
\mathrm{T}
-
yardstick
is any interpretation
\mathcal{I}
satisfying all the conditions below.

(YNom):

\mathsf{N}_{\text{\faIcon{ruler}}}^{\mathrm{T}}
-named elements in
\mathcal{I}
are pairwise different and
(\mathit{r}+\mathit{s})^{*}
-reachable from
\texttt{st}^{\mathcal{I}}
.

(YNoLoop):

No
\mathsf{N}_{\text{\faIcon{ruler}}}^{\mathrm{T}}
-named element can
(\mathit{r}+\mathit{s})^{+}
-reach itself.

(YMid):

\texttt{md}^{\mathcal{I}}
is the
unique
element with an
\mathit{s}
-successor that is
\mathit{r}^{*}
-reachable from
\texttt{st}^{\mathcal{I}}
.

(YSuccOfMid):

The
\mathit{s}
-successors of
\texttt{md}^{\mathcal{I}}
are precisely
\{\texttt{md}_{\mathrm{t}}\mid\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}\}
-named elements.

(YReachMidT):

For every
\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}
we have that
\texttt{md}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathcal{I}}
can
\mathit{s}^{*}
-reach
\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathcal{I}}
but it cannot
\mathit{s}^{*}
-reach
\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{I}}
for all
\mathrm{t}^{\prime}\neq\mathrm{t}
.

(YEqDst):

The elem.
{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}
-reachable from
\texttt{st}^{\mathcal{I}}
are precisely the
\{\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}\mid\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}\}
-named ones.

(YNoEqDst):

No
\{\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}\mid\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}\}
-named element is
{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}
-reachable from an element
(\mathit{s}+\mathit{r})^{+}
-reachable from
\texttt{st}^{\mathcal{I}}
.

An example
\{\heartsuit,\spadesuit\}
-yardstick is depicted below.
A “minimal” yardstick contains the grey nodes only.

0
\texttt{st}^{\mathcal{I}}
0
\texttt{md}^{\mathcal{I}}
0
\texttt{md}_{\heartsuit}^{\mathcal{I}}
0
\texttt{end}_{\heartsuit}^{\mathcal{I}}
0
\texttt{md}_{\spadesuit}^{\mathcal{I}}
0
\texttt{end}_{\spadesuit}^{\mathcal{I}}
\mathit{r}
\mathit{r}
\mathit{r}
\mathit{r}
\mathit{r}
\mathit{r}
\mathit{r}
\mathit{r}
\mathit{r}
\mathit{r}
\mathit{r}
\mathit{r}
\mathit{r}
\mathit{r}
\mathit{s}
\mathit{s}
\mathit{s}
\mathit{s}
\mathit{s}
\mathit{s}
\mathit{s}
\mathit{s}
\mathit{s}
\mathit{s}
\mathit{s}

The forthcoming lemma explains the name “yardstick”. Intuitively it says that in any
\mathrm{T}
-yardstick
\mathcal{I}
, all
\mathit{s}^{*}
-paths from
\texttt{md}^{\mathcal{I}}
to all
\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathcal{I}}
have equal length.

Lemma 11
.

Let
\mathcal{I}
be a
\mathrm{T}
-yardstick.
Then there exists a unique positive integer
\mathrm{N}
such that:
(i) for all
\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}
we have that
\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathcal{I}}
is
\mathit{s}^{\mathrm{N}}
-reachable from
\texttt{md}^{\mathcal{I}}
, and
(ii) for all
\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}
we have that
\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathcal{I}}
is
\mathit{s}^{\mathrm{N}{-}1}
-reachable from
\texttt{md}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathcal{I}}
.
We will call
\mathrm{N}
the
length
of
\mathcal{I}
.

Proof 4.4
.

Fix
\mathrm{t}_{\star}\in\mathrm{T}
.
By
(YEqDst):
we know that
\texttt{st}^{\mathcal{I}}

{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}
-reaches
\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}_{\star}}^{\mathcal{I}}
, and let
\rho\coloneqq\rho_{1}\ldots\rho_{2\mathrm{N}{+}1}
be a path witnessing it.
We claim that this is the desired length of
\mathcal{I}
.
First, note that
\mathrm{N}>0
by
(YNom):
.
Second, by the semantics of
{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}
, for all
i\leq\mathrm{N}
we have
(\rho_{i},\rho_{i{+}1})\in\mathit{r}^{\mathcal{I}}
and
(\rho_{\mathrm{N}{+}i},\rho_{\mathrm{N}{+}i{+}1})\in\mathit{s}^{\mathcal{I}}
.
Thus
\rho_{\mathrm{N}{+}1}
is
\mathit{r}^{*}
-reachable from
\texttt{st}^{\mathcal{I}}
and has an
\mathit{s}
-successor.
These two facts imply (by
(YMid):
) that
\rho_{\mathrm{N}{+}1}
is equal to
\texttt{md}^{\mathcal{I}}
.
It remains to show that all the paths leading from
\texttt{md}^{\mathcal{I}}
to some
\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}
are of length
\mathrm{N}
.
Towards a contradiction, assume that there is
\mathrm{t}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{T}
and an integer
\mathrm{M}\neq\mathrm{N}
such that
\texttt{md}^{\mathcal{I}}

\mathit{s}^{\mathrm{M}}
-reaches
\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{I}}
via a path
\rho^{\prime}\coloneqq\rho_{1}^{\prime}\ldots\rho_{\mathrm{M}}^{\prime}
.
We stress that
\rho_{1}^{\prime}=\texttt{md}^{\mathcal{I}}
and
\rho_{\mathrm{M}}^{\prime}=\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{I}}
(by design of
\rho^{\prime}
), and
\rho_{2}^{\prime}=\texttt{md}_{\mathrm{t}^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{I}}
(by a conjunction of
(YSuccOfMid):
and
(YReachMidT):
).
To conclude the proof, it suffices to resolve the following two cases.

•

Suppose that
\mathrm{M}<\mathrm{N}
.
Then
\rho_{\mathrm{N}{+}1{-}\mathrm{M}}

(\mathit{r}^{\mathrm{M}}\mathit{s}^{\mathrm{M}})
-reaches (thus also
{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}
-reaches)
\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{I}}
, as witnessed by the path
\rho_{\mathrm{N}{+}1{-}\mathrm{M}}\ldots\rho_{\mathrm{N}}\rho^{\prime}
.
Moreover
\rho_{\mathrm{N}{+}1{-}\mathrm{M}}
is
\mathit{r}^{+}
-reachable from
\texttt{st}^{\mathcal{I}}
, witnessed by the path
\rho_{1}\ldots\rho_{\mathrm{N}{+}1{-}\mathrm{M}}
(note that its length is positive by the inequality
\mathrm{M}<\mathrm{N}
).
This yields a contradiction with
(YNoEqDst):
.

•

Suppose that
\mathrm{M}>\mathrm{N}
.
Consider a path
\rho_{1}\ldots\rho_{\mathrm{N}}\rho_{1}^{\prime}\ldots\rho_{\mathrm{N}}^{\prime}
.
By construction, such a path witnesses the fact that
\texttt{st}^{\mathcal{I}}

(\mathit{r}^{\mathrm{N}}\mathit{s}^{\mathrm{N}})
-reaches (and thus also
{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}
-reaches)
\rho_{\mathrm{N}}^{\prime}
.
By
(YEqDst):
we infer that
\rho_{\mathrm{N}}^{\prime}
is then
\{\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}\mid\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}\}
-named.
As
\rho_{2}^{\prime}=\texttt{md}_{\mathrm{t}^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{I}}

\mathit{s}^{+}
-reaches
\rho_{\mathrm{N}}^{\prime}
, we infer that
\rho_{\mathrm{N}}^{\prime}=\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{I}}
(otherwise we would have a contradiction with
(YReachMidT):
).
But then
\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{I}}

\mathit{s}^{+}
-reaches itself via a path
\rho_{\mathrm{N}}^{\prime}\ldots\rho_{\mathrm{M}}
, which is of positive length by the fact that
\mathrm{M}>\mathrm{N}
.
This yields a contradiction with
(YNoLoop):
.

This establishes Property (i). Property (ii) is now immediate by
(YSuccOfMid):
.

The next lemma proves the existence of arbitrary large yardsticks.

Lemma 12
.

For every finite non-empty set
\mathrm{T}
and a positive integer
\mathrm{N}
, there exists a
\mathrm{T}
-yardstick of length
\mathrm{N}
.

Proof 4.5
.

Consider the following interpretation
\mathcal{I}
with
\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\coloneqq\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{N}}\cup\{\mathrm{N}\}\cup(\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{N}{-}1}\times\mathrm{T})
:

•

\texttt{st}^{\mathcal{I}}\coloneqq 0
,
\texttt{md}^{\mathcal{I}}\coloneqq\mathrm{N}
,
(\texttt{md}_{\mathrm{t}})^{\mathcal{I}}\coloneqq(0,\mathrm{t})
,
(\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}})^{\mathcal{I}}\coloneqq(\mathrm{N}{-}1,\mathrm{t})
for all
\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}
, and
\texttt{a}^{\mathcal{I}}\coloneqq 0
for all names
\texttt{a}\in\mathbf{N_{I}}\setminus\mathsf{N}_{\text{\faIcon{ruler}}}^{\mathrm{T}}
.

•

\mathit{r}^{\mathcal{I}}\coloneqq\{(i,i{+}1)\mid i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{N}}\}
,

\mathit{s}^{\mathcal{I}}\coloneqq\{(\mathrm{N},(0,\mathrm{t})),((i,\mathrm{t}),(i{+}1,\mathrm{t}))\mid\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T},i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{N}{-}1}\}
, and
(\mathit{r}^{\prime})^{\mathcal{I}}=\emptyset
for all role names
\mathit{r}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{N_{R}}\setminus\{\mathit{r},\mathit{s}\}
.

An example such
\mathcal{I}
for
\mathrm{N}=3
and
\mathrm{T}=\{\heartsuit,\spadesuit\}
is depicted above (in restriction to grey nodes only).
It is routine to verify that
\mathcal{I}
satisfies all the properties in Definition
4
, as well as conditions (i) and (ii) from the statement of Lemma
11
.
This concludes the proof.

To make use of yardsticks in our proofs, we need to axiomatise them inside
\mathcal{ALCO}_{\textsf{reg}}^{\mathit{r}\#\mathit{s}\#}
.

Lemma 13
.

There exists an
\mathcal{ALCO}_{\textsf{reg}}^{\mathit{r}\#\mathit{s}\#}
-concept
\mathrm{C}_{\text{\faIcon{ruler}}}^{\mathrm{T}}
, that employs only role names
\mathit{r},\mathit{s}
and individual names from
\mathsf{N}_{\text{\faIcon{ruler}}}^{\mathrm{T}}
, such that for all interpretations
\mathcal{I}
we have:
\mathcal{I}
is a
\mathrm{T}
-yardstick if and only if
\mathcal{I}
is a model of
\mathrm{C}_{\text{\faIcon{ruler}}}^{\mathrm{T}}
.

Proof 4.6
.

The following concepts are written from the point of view of the interpretation of
st
.

\displaystyle\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{\ref{yardstick:DifNom}}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathop{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}}_{\texttt{a}\in\mathsf{N}_{\text{\faIcon{ruler}}}^{\mathrm{T}}}\exists{(\mathit{r}+\mathit{s})^{*}}\Big[\{\texttt{a}\}\sqcap\mathop{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}}_{\texttt{b}\in\mathsf{N}_{\text{\faIcon{ruler}}}^{\mathrm{T}}\setminus\{\texttt{a}\}}\neg\{\texttt{b}\}\Big].

\displaystyle\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{\ref{yardstick:NoLoop}}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathop{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}}_{\texttt{a}\in\mathsf{N}_{\text{\faIcon{ruler}}}^{\mathrm{T}}}\forall{(\mathit{r}+\mathit{s})^{*}}.\Big[\{\texttt{a}\}\to\forall{(\mathit{r}+\mathit{s})^{+}}.\neg\{\texttt{a}\}\Big].

\displaystyle\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{\ref{yardstick:Mid}}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Big[\exists{\mathit{r}^{*}}.\left((\exists{\mathit{s}}.\top)\sqcap\{\texttt{md}\}\right)\Big]\sqcap\Big[\forall{\mathit{r}^{*}}.\left((\exists{\mathit{s}}.\top)\to\{\texttt{md}\}\right)\Big].

\displaystyle\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{\ref{yardstick:SuccOfMid}}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\forall{(\mathit{r}+\mathit{s})^{*}}.\left(\{\texttt{md}\}\to\Big[\mathop{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}}_{\texttt{a}\in\{\texttt{md}_{\mathrm{t}}\mid\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}\}}\exists{\mathit{s}}.\{\texttt{a}\}\Big]\sqcap\Big[\forall{\mathit{s}}.\bigsqcup_{\texttt{a}\in\{\texttt{md}_{\mathrm{t}}\mid\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}\}}\{\texttt{a}\}\Big]\right).

\displaystyle\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{\ref{yardstick:ReachMidT}}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathop{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}}_{\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}}\forall{(\mathit{r}+\mathit{s})^{*}}.\{\texttt{md}_{\mathrm{t}}\}\to\Big[\left(\exists{\mathit{s}^{*}}.\{\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}\}\right)\sqcap\mathop{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}}_{\mathrm{t}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{T},\mathrm{t}\neq\mathrm{t}^{\prime}}\forall{\mathit{s}^{*}}.\neg\{\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}^{\prime}}\}\Big].

\displaystyle\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{\ref{yardstick:EqDist}}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left(\mathop{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}}_{\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}}\exists{{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}}.\{\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}\}\right)\sqcap\forall{{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}}.\left(\bigsqcup_{\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}}\{\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}\}\right).

\displaystyle\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{\ref{yardstick:NoEqDist}}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\forall{(\mathit{r}+\mathit{s})^{+}}.\forall{{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}}.\left(\mathop{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}}_{\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}}\neg\{\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}\}\right).

We define
\mathrm{C}_{\text{\faIcon{ruler}}}^{\mathrm{T}}
as the conjunction of
\{\texttt{st}\}
and all the concept definitions presented above.
By semantics of
\mathcal{ALCO}_{\textsf{reg}}^{\mathit{r}\#\mathit{s}\#}
we have that
\mathrm{C}_{\text{\faIcon{ruler}}}^{\mathrm{T}}
is consistent if and only if
(\mathrm{C}_{\text{\faIcon{ruler}}}^{\mathrm{T}})^{\mathcal{I}}=\{\texttt{st}^{\mathcal{I}}\}
.

We next put pseudosnakes and yardsticks together, obtaining metricobras.
The intuition behind their construction is fairly simple: (i) we take a disjoint union of a pseudosnake and a yardstick, (ii) we then connect (via the role
\mathit{s}
) every element carrying a tile
\mathrm{t}
with the interpretation of the corresponding nominal
\texttt{md}_{\mathrm{t}}
, and finally (iii) we synchronise the length of the underlying yardstick, say
\mathrm{N}
, with the length of the path between the interpretations of
ld
and
rd
.
After such “merging”, retrieving
(SVerti):
is easy: rather than testing if every
\mathrm{N}
-reachable element from some
\mathrm{d}
carries a suitable tile
\mathrm{t}
(for an a priori unknown
\mathrm{N}
) we can check instead whether
\mathrm{d}
can
{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}
-reach the interpretation of
\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}
.

A formal definition and a picture come next.

\mathit{r}

Figure 2.
A fragment of an example
\mathdutchcal{D}
-metricobra representing
\xi
from Example
4
.
The upper part corresponds to a
\mathdutchcal{D}
-snake, and the lower part corresponds to a
\mathrm{T}
-yardstick.
The distances between named elements are important.

{defi}

Let
\mathdutchcal{D}\coloneqq(\mathrm{Col},\mathrm{T},\hbox to7.07pt{\vbox to7.07pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-67.59889pt\lower-60.43852pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 93.81 -83.35 h 9.22 v 9.22 h -9.22 Z}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{71.1319pt}{-56.90552pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{62}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}})
be a domino tiling system and
cbra
be an individual name.
An interpretation
\mathcal{I}
is a
\mathdutchcal{D}
-
metricobra
if all the conditions below are satisfied:

(MInit):

\mathcal{I}
is a
\mathdutchcal{D}
-pseudosnake and a
\mathrm{T}
-yardstick, and
\texttt{cbra}^{\mathcal{I}}
has precisely two successors: one
\mathit{r}
-successor, namely
\texttt{ld}^{\mathcal{I}}
, and one
\mathit{s}
-successor, namely
\texttt{st}^{\mathcal{I}}
.

(MTile):

For every tile
\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}
and every element
\mathrm{d}\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
that is
\mathit{r}^{*}
-reachable from
\texttt{ld}^{\mathcal{I}}
we have that
\mathrm{d}
carries a tile
\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}
if and only if
\mathrm{d}
has a unique
\mathit{s}
-successor and such a successor is equal to
\texttt{md}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathcal{I}}
.

(MSync):

Let
\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}
be the tile labelling
\texttt{rd}^{\mathcal{I}}
.
Then (a)
\texttt{cbra}^{\mathcal{I}}

{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}
-reaches
\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathcal{I}}
and cannot
{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}
-reach any of
\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}^{\prime}}^{\mathcal{I}}
for
\mathrm{t}^{\prime}\neq\mathrm{t}
,
(b)
\texttt{cbra}^{\mathcal{I}}
cannot
{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}
-reach an element that can
\mathit{s}^{+}
-reach
\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathcal{I}}
,
(c) no element
\mathit{r}^{*}
-reachable from
\texttt{ld}^{\mathcal{I}}
can
{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}
-reach
\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathcal{I}}
.

(MVerti):

For all elements
\mathrm{d}
that are
\mathit{r}^{*}
-reachable from
\texttt{ld}^{\mathcal{I}}
and are labelled by some
\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}
that is not up-border, we have that there exists a tile
\mathrm{t}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{T}
such that (a)
\mathrm{t}
and
\mathrm{t}^{\prime}
are
\mathrm{V}
-compatible, (b)
\mathrm{t}
is left-border (resp. right-border) iff
\mathrm{t}^{\prime}
is, and (c)
\mathrm{d}
can
{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}
-reach
\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}^{\prime}}
but cannot reach
{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}
-reach
\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}^{\prime\prime}}
for all
\mathrm{t}^{\prime\prime}\neq\mathrm{t}^{\prime}
.

We first show that
\mathdutchcal{D}
-metricobras are axiomatizable in
\mathcal{ALCO}_{\textsf{reg}}^{\mathit{r}\#\mathit{s}\#}
.

Lemma 14
.

There exists an
\mathcal{ALCO}_{\textsf{reg}}^{\mathit{r}\#\mathit{s}\#}
-concept
\mathrm{C}_{\includegraphics[scale]{graphics/cobra.png}}{D}
such that for all interpretations
\mathcal{I}
we have that
\mathcal{I}
is a
\mathdutchcal{D}
-metricobra if and only if
(\mathrm{C}_{\includegraphics[scale]{graphics/cobra.png}}{D})^{\mathcal{I}}=\{\texttt{cbra}^{\mathcal{I}}\}
.

Proof 4.7
.

We present a rather straightforward axiomatization of the aforementioned properties, written from the point of view of the interpretation of
cbra
.
Note that as the interpretation of
cbra
has a unique
\mathit{r}
-successor, we can simplify concepts of the form
\forall{\mathit{r}}.[\{\texttt{ld}\}\to(\forall{\mathit{r}^{*}}.\mathrm{C})]
to
\forall{\mathit{r}^{+}}.\mathrm{C}
, which we frequently do below.

\displaystyle\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{\ref{metricobra:Init}}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\exists{\mathit{r}}.\mathrm{C}_{\includegraphics[scale]{graphics/icons8-snake-30.png}}{D}\sqcap\exists{\mathit{s}}.\mathrm{C}_{\text{\faIcon{ruler}}}^{\mathrm{T}}\sqcap\forall{\mathit{r}}.(\{\texttt{ld}\}\sqcap\neg\{\texttt{st}\})\sqcap\forall{\mathit{s}}.(\neg\{\texttt{ld}\}\sqcap\{\texttt{st}\}).

\displaystyle\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{\ref{metricobra:Tiles}}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathop{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}}_{\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}}\forall{\mathit{r}^{+}}.\left(\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{t}}\leftrightarrow[\exists{\mathit{s}}.\{\texttt{md}_{\mathrm{t}}\}\sqcap\forall{\mathit{s}}.\{\texttt{md}_{\mathrm{t}}\}]\right).

\displaystyle\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{\ref{metricobra:Verti}}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\forall{\mathit{r}^{+}}.\mathop{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}}_{\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}}^{\text{not up-border}}\left(\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{t}}\to\hskip-15.00002pt\bigsqcup_{\mathrm{t}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{T}}^{\text{sat.\ (a),(b) of }\mathrm{\ref{metricobra:Verti}}}\hskip-30.00005pt\big[(\exists{{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}}.\{\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}^{\prime}}\})\sqcap\hskip-10.00002pt\mathop{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}}_{\mathrm{t}^{\prime\prime}\neq\mathrm{t}^{\prime},\mathrm{t}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathrm{T}}\hskip-5.0pt\forall{{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}}.\neg\{\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}^{\prime\prime}}\}\big]\right).

\!\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{\ref{metricobra:Sync}}}^{\mathrm{t}}\!\coloneqq\!(\exists{{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}}.\{\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}\}\hskip-1.0pt)\sqcap\mathop{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}}_{\mathrm{t}^{\prime}\neq\mathrm{t}}(\forall{{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}}.\neg\{\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}^{\prime}}\}\hskip-1.0pt)\sqcap(\forall{{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}}.\forall{\mathit{s}^{+}}.\neg\{\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}\}\hskip-1.0pt)\sqcap\forall{\mathit{r}^{+}}.\forall{{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}}.\neg\{\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}\}.

We define
\mathrm{C}_{\includegraphics[scale]{graphics/cobra.png}}{D}\coloneqq\{\texttt{cbra}\}\sqcap\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{\ref{metricobra:Init}}}\sqcap\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{\ref{metricobra:Tiles}}}\sqcap\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{\ref{metricobra:Verti}}}\sqcap\mathop{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}}_{\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}}[\left(\exists{\mathit{r}^{+}}.(\{\texttt{rd}\}\sqcap\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{t}})\right)\to\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{\ref{metricobra:Sync}}}^{\mathrm{t}}].

Its correctness follows from the semantics of
\mathcal{ALCO}_{\textsf{reg}}^{\mathit{r}\#\mathit{s}\#}
and Lemmas
10
and
13
.

As the second step, we show that for every
\mathdutchcal{D}
-snake we can construct a
\mathdutchcal{D}
-metricobra.

Lemma 15
.

If
\mathcal{I}
is a
\mathdutchcal{D}
-snake then there exists a
\mathdutchcal{D}
-metricobra
\mathcal{J}
.

Proof 4.8
.

Let
\mathcal{I}
be a
\mathdutchcal{D}
-snake,
\mathrm{N}
be the integer guaranteed by
(SLen):
, and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
be any
\mathrm{T}
-yardstick of length
\mathrm{N}
(existence guaranteed by Lemma
12
).
We construct an interpretation
\mathcal{J}
as a disjoint union of
\mathcal{I}
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
and an additional domain element that we interpret as
\texttt{cbra}^{\mathcal{J}}
.
We let
\mathcal{J}
interpret all names from
\mathsf{N}_{\text{\faIcon{ruler}}}^{\mathrm{T}}
as in
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
, all names from
\mathsf{N}_{\text{\faIcon{puzzle-piece}}}^{\mathrm{T}}
as in
\mathcal{I}
, and all other (unused in our concept definitions) names as
\texttt{cbra}^{\mathcal{J}}
.
Interpretation of concept names is inherited from
\mathcal{I}
and
\mathcal{J}
.
Finally we interpret role names as in
\mathcal{I}
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
with minor corrections.
More precisely:
(i) we alter the interpretation of
\mathit{r}^{\mathcal{J}}
to include an extra pair
(\texttt{cbra}^{\mathcal{J}},\texttt{ld}^{\mathcal{J}})
, and
(ii) we alter the interpretation of
\mathit{s}^{\mathcal{J}}
to include the pair
(\texttt{cbra}^{\mathcal{J}},\texttt{st}^{\mathcal{J}})
and
\bigcup_{\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}}\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathcal{J}}\times\{\texttt{md}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathcal{J}}\}
.
Consult Figure
2
to see an example construction of
\mathcal{J}
from
\mathcal{I}
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
.
The satisfaction of properties
(MInit):
and
(MTile):
follow from the construction of
\mathcal{J}
, while the other two properties are due to, respectively,
(SLen):
and
(SVerti):
.
Thus
\mathcal{J}
is the desired metricobra.

Finally, we show that every
\mathdutchcal{D}
-metricobra is actually a
\mathdutchcal{D}
-snake.

Lemma 16
.

If
\mathcal{I}
is a
\mathdutchcal{D}
-metricobra then it is also a
\mathdutchcal{D}
-snake.

Proof 4.9
.

As
\mathcal{I}
is a pseudosnake by definition, it suffices to show that it satisfies the missing conditions of Definition
4
.
Our first goal is to establish
\mathcal{I}\models

(SLen):
.
Note that by
(SSpecTil):
we have that
\texttt{rd}^{\mathcal{I}}
is the only element
\mathit{r}^{*}
-reachable from
\texttt{ld}^{\mathcal{I}}
that carries a down- and right-border tile, say
\mathrm{t}
.
Furthermore by
(MTile):
and
(YReachMidT):
we infer that
\texttt{rd}^{\mathcal{I}}
is the only element
\mathit{r}^{*}
-reachable from
\texttt{ld}^{\mathcal{I}}
that can
\mathit{s}^{*}
-reach
\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathcal{I}}
.
Take
\mathrm{N}
to be the length of the yardstick.
By Lemma
11
we know that
\texttt{md}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathcal{I}}

\mathit{s}^{\mathrm{N}{-}1}
-reaches
\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathcal{I}}
, hence
\texttt{rd}^{\mathcal{I}}

\mathit{s}^{\mathrm{N}}
-reaches
\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathcal{I}}
(and there is no other integer
\mathrm{M}\neq\mathrm{N}
for which such reachability conditions hold).
From
(MSync):
we know that
\texttt{cbra}^{\mathcal{I}}

{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}
-reaches
\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathcal{I}}
, thus by previous observations we deduce that
\texttt{cbra}^{\mathcal{I}}

\mathit{r}^{\mathrm{N}}\mathit{s}^{\mathrm{N}}
-reaches
\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathcal{I}}
(whence
\texttt{ld}^{\mathcal{I}}

\mathit{r}^{\mathrm{N}{-}1}
-reaches
\texttt{rd}^{\mathcal{I}}
).
This establishes the existence of a path of length
\mathrm{N}
mentioned in
(SLen):
, and we next need to show that all such paths have equal length.
Consider cases:

•

There exists
\mathrm{M}<\mathrm{N}
such that
\texttt{ld}^{\mathcal{I}}

\mathit{r}^{\mathrm{M}{-}1}
-reaches
\texttt{rd}^{\mathcal{I}}
.
Then
\texttt{cbra}^{\mathcal{I}}

\mathit{r}^{\mathrm{M}}\mathit{s}^{\mathrm{M}}
-reaches some element that can
\mathit{s}^{+}
-reach
\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathcal{I}}
. This yields a contradiction with condition (b) of
(MSync):
.

•

There exists
\mathrm{M}>\mathrm{N}
such that
\texttt{ld}^{\mathcal{I}}

\mathit{r}^{\mathrm{M}{-}1}
-reaches
\texttt{rd}^{\mathcal{I}}
.
Then there is an element
\mathit{r}^{*}
-reachable from
\texttt{ld}^{\mathcal{I}}
that can
\mathit{r}^{\mathrm{N}}\mathit{s}^{\mathrm{N}}
-reach
\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathcal{I}}
. This contradictions condition (c) of
(MSync):
.

Hence
\mathrm{N}
is indeed unique.
The fact that
\texttt{rd}^{\mathcal{I}}
is the only element
\mathit{r}^{\mathrm{N}{-}1}
-reachable from
\texttt{ld}^{\mathcal{I}}
follows from the uniqueness of the tile assigned to
\texttt{rd}^{\mathcal{I}}
, see
(SSpecTil):
and Property (a) of
(MSync):
.
It remains now to show that
\mathcal{I}\models

(SVerti):
. To do so, it suffices to observe that the following property holds.
As all the
\mathit{s}^{*}
-paths from an element carrying a tile
\mathrm{t}
to
\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}
are of length
\mathrm{N}
(by the previous discussion and Lemma
11
), we can see that
{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}
-reachability of
\texttt{end}_{\mathrm{t}}
is equivalent to
\mathit{r}^{\mathrm{N}}
-reachability of some element carrying
\mathrm{t}
.
Then the satisfaction of
(SVerti):
follows immediately by
(MVerti):
.
Hence,
\mathcal{I}
is indeed a
\mathdutchcal{D}
-snake.

By collecting all previous lemmas we establish the correspondence between solvability of tiling systems and satisfiability of
\mathrm{C}_{\includegraphics[scale]{graphics/cobra.png}}{D}
.

Lemma 17
.

A domino tiling system
\mathdutchcal{D}
is solvable if and only if
\mathrm{C}_{\includegraphics[scale]{graphics/cobra.png}}{D}
has a model.

Proof 4.10
.

If
\mathdutchcal{D}
is solvable, then by Lemma
8
there exists a
\mathdutchcal{D}
-snake, which by Lemma
15
implies the existence of a
\mathdutchcal{D}
-metricobra, which is a model of
\mathrm{C}_{\includegraphics[scale]{graphics/cobra.png}}{D}
(see Lemma
14
).
For the other direction, if
\mathrm{C}_{\includegraphics[scale]{graphics/cobra.png}}{D}
has a model, then such a model is a
\mathdutchcal{D}
-metricobra (by Lemma
14
), as well as a
\mathdutchcal{D}
-snake (by Lemma
16
).
As the existence of a
\mathdutchcal{D}
-snake guarantees that
\mathdutchcal{D}
is solvable by Lemma
9
, this finishes the proof.

Hence, we can conclude the main theorem of the paper.

Theorem 18
.

The concept satisfiability problem for
\mathcal{ALCO}_{\textsf{vpl}}
is undecidable, even if the languages allowable in concepts are restricted to
\{\mathit{r},\mathit{s},\mathit{r}^{+},\mathit{s}^{+},\mathit{r}^{*},\mathit{s}^{*},(\mathit{r}+\mathit{s})^{*},(\mathit{r}+\mathit{s})^{+},{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}\}
.

The logic
\mathcal{ALCO}_{\textsf{reg}}
is a notational variant of Propositional Dynamic Logic with nominals
[
KS14
]
.
Thus the above theorem provides results also in the realm of formal verification.

5.
Negative results III: Entailment of queries with non-regular atoms

We conclude the negative part of the paper by showing that positive results regarding entailment of conjunctive queries with visibly-pushdown atoms in the database setting
[
LL15
, Thm. 2]
do not generalise even to
\mathcal{ALC}
ontologies.
We provide a reduction from the
White-bordered Octant Tiling Problem
, which we are going to define next.
Roughly speaking, the ontology used in our reduction will define a “grid” covered with tiles, while the query counterpart will serve as a tool to detect mismatches in its lower triangle (a.k.a. octant).

We first show undecidability of the White-bordered Octant Tiling Problem, which follows by a straightforward reduction from the Octant Tiling Problem
[
BDG+10
, Sec. 3.1]
.

Lemma 19
.

The white-bordered Octant Tiling Problem is undecidable.

Proof 5.2
.

We say that a domino tiling system
\mathdutchcal{D}

almost covers

\xi\colon\mathbb{O}\to\mathrm{T}
such that all pairs

fulfil

and
(OHori):
.
It was stated by Bresolin et al.
[
BDG+10
, Sec. 3.1]
(and follows from the classical work of van Emde Boas
[
Boa97
]
) that deciding whether

.
We claim that
\mathdutchcal{D}^{\prime}
covers the octant if and only if
\mathdutchcal{D}
almost covers the octant. Indeed:

•

The “if” direction is relatively straightforward.
Let
\xi
be a map witnessing that
\mathdutchcal{D}
almost covers the octant.
We alter the tiles assigned by
\xi
to the diagonal to make them left- and up-border, then we shift
\xi
right, and fill the remaining places with
.

be defined as: (i)

, (ii)

for all remaining

.
It can be readily verified that
\xi^{\prime}
satisfies the required conditions.

•

For the “only if” direction, let
\xi^{\prime}
be a map witnessing that
\mathdutchcal{D}^{\prime}
covers the octant. It suffices to take
\xi\colon(i,j)\mapsto\xi^{\prime}(i{+}2,j)
. By Observation
5
) none of the tiles assigned by
\xi
is white-bordered. As
\xi^{\prime}
covers the octant, we infer that
\xi
(almost) covers the octant.

Our undecidability proof relies on concepts from
\mathrm{C}_{\text{\faIcon{puzzle-piece}}}^{\mathrm{T}}
and the non-regular language
{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}
.
We fix and enumerate a set of tiles
\mathrm{T}
as
\mathrm{t}_{1},\ldots,\mathrm{t}_{\mathrm{N}}
for
\mathrm{N}\coloneqq|\mathrm{T}|
.
As the first building block, we introduce octant interpretations.
An interpretation
\mathcal{I}
is called
\mathrm{T}
-
octant
if there exists a function
\xi\colon\mathbb{O}\to\mathrm{T}
fulling
(OInit):
and
(OVerti):
(but not necessarily
(OHori):
) that satisfies the following conditions:
(i)
\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}=\mathbb{O}
,
(ii)
\mathit{r}^{\mathcal{I}}=\{((n,0),(n{+}1,0))\mid n\in\mathbb{N}\}
,
(iii)
\mathit{s}^{\mathcal{I}}=\{((n,m),(n,m{+}1))\mid n,m\in\mathbb{N},m<n\}
, and
(iv)
\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{t}}^{\mathcal{I}}=\{(n,m)\mid\xi(n,m)=\mathrm{t}\}
for all tiles
\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}
.
In this case
\mathcal{I}

represents

\xi
.
Due to the fact that
\xi
is a function, every domain element carries precisely one tile.
Moreover, for every such
\xi
we can easily find a
\mathrm{T}
-octant representing
\xi
(just employ the above definition).
For more intuitions consult Figure
3
.

To avoid disjunction in the forthcoming query, we need to extend octant interpretations with yet another way of representing tiles, which will be based on distances.
Suppose that an element
(n,m)
from an octant interpretation
\mathcal{I}
carries a tile
\mathrm{t}_{i}
, and that the tile assigned to its horizontal predecessor
(n{-}1,m)
, if exists, is equal to
\mathrm{t}_{j}
.
We equip
(n,m)
with an outgoing
\mathit{s}^{+}
-path
\rho
of length
\mathrm{N}
, composed of fresh elements (we employ fresh concept names
\mathrm{In}
and
\overline{\mathrm{In}}
to make a distinction between elements in octant and the ones present in “extra paths”).
The
i
-th element of
\rho
will be the unique element of
\rho
that belongs to the interpretation of a concept
\mathrm{Cur}
.
Similarly, the
j
-th element of
\rho
will be the unique element of
\rho
that belongs to the interpretation of a concept
\mathrm{Prev}
.
Thus, the distance from
(n,m)
to an element labelled by the concept
\mathrm{Cur}
(resp.
\mathrm{Prev}
) uniquely determines the tile of a current node (resp. its horizontal predecessor).
As a mere technicality, needed for query design, we enrich the element
(0,0)
with an incoming
\mathit{r}^{+}
-path of length
\mathrm{N}
.
A formalization comes next.

{defi}

Let
\mathcal{I}
be an interpretation with a domain
\mathbb{O}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{N}{+}1}\cup\{({-}i{-}1,0,0)\mid i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{N}}\}
,
and let
\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{O}}
be the restriction of
\mathcal{I}
to the set
\mathbb{O}\times\{0\}
.
We call
\mathcal{I}
a
\mathrm{T}
-
hyperoctant
if:

•

\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{O}}
is isomorphic (via a projection
(n,m,0)\mapsto(n,m)
) to a
\mathrm{T}
-octant interpretation,

•

\mathrm{In}^{\mathcal{I}}=\mathbb{O}\times\{0\}
,
\overline{\mathrm{In}}^{\mathcal{I}}=\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\setminus\mathrm{In}^{\mathcal{I}}
,
\overline{\mathrm{Prev}}^{\mathcal{I}}=\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\setminus\mathrm{Prev}^{\mathcal{I}}
,

•

\mathit{r}^{\mathcal{I}}=\mathit{r}^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{O}}}\cup\{(({-}i{-}1,0,0),({-}i,0,0))\mid i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{N}}\}
,

•

\mathit{s}^{\mathcal{I}}=\mathit{s}^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{O}}}\cup\{((n,m,k),(n,m,k{+}1))\mid(n,m)\in\mathbb{O},k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{N}}\}
,

•

\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{t}_{k}}^{\mathcal{I}}=\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{t}_{k}}^{\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{O}}}
and
\mathrm{Cur}^{\mathcal{I}}=\{(n,m,k)\mid(n,m,0)\in\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{t}_{k}}^{\mathcal{I}},\mathrm{t}_{k}\in\mathrm{T}\}
, and

•

for every
(n,m)\in\mathbb{O}
there is precisely one positive
k
for which
(n,m,k)\in\mathrm{Prev}^{\mathcal{I}}
holds, and for such a number
k
we have that
\mathrm{t}_{k}
and the tile carried by
(n,m,0)
are
\mathrm{H}
-compatible.

Note that, in addition to what is present in the definition of
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctant, we employed fresh concept names, namely
\mathrm{In}
,
\mathrm{Prev}
,
\mathrm{Cur}
,
\overline{\mathrm{In}}
, and
\overline{\mathrm{Prev}}
.
The purpose of “overlined” concepts is to help with a design of a query, as the use of negation is not allowed there.
We call
\mathcal{I}

proper
if for all
(n,m,k)\in\mathrm{Cur}^{\mathcal{I}}
we have
(n{+}1,m,k)\in\mathrm{Prev}^{\mathcal{I}}
.
Note that properness is not definable in
\mathcal{ALC}
, but we will enforce it with a query.
The map
\xi\colon\mathbb{O}\to\mathrm{T}

represented
by a hyperoctant
\mathcal{I}
is the map represented by its
\mathrm{T}
-octant substructure
\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{O}}
.

We next relate proper
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctants and domino tiling systems.

Lemma 20
.

Let
\mathdutchcal{D}\coloneqq(\mathrm{Col},\mathrm{T},\hbox to7.07pt{\vbox to7.07pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-67.59889pt\lower-60.43852pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 93.81 -83.35 h 9.22 v 9.22 h -9.22 Z}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{71.1319pt}{-56.90552pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{62}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}})
be a domino tilling system.
For every proper
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctant
\mathcal{I}
, the map
\xi\colon\mathbb{O}\to\mathrm{T}
represented by
\mathcal{I}
witnesses that
\mathdutchcal{D}
covers the octant.
If
\mathdutchcal{D}
covers the octant, as witnessed by a map
\xi\colon\mathbb{O}\to\mathrm{T}
, then there exists a proper
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctant
\mathcal{I}
representing
\xi
.

Proof 5.3
.

Take a proper
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctant
\mathcal{I}
, and the map
\xi
represented by
\mathcal{I}
.
By definition of a
\mathrm{T}
-octant, we have that
\xi
satisfies
(OInit):
and
(OVerti):
.
To establish
(OHori):
take any
(n,m)\in\mathbb{O}
, and suppose that
\xi(n,m)=\mathrm{t}_{i}
and
\xi(n{+}1,m)=\mathrm{t}_{j}
hold for some integers
i,j\in\mathbb{N}
.
By definition of a hyperoctant and properness of
\mathcal{I}
, we have that
(n{+}1,m,0)
carries the tile
\mathrm{t}_{j}
, and
(n{+}1,m,i)\in\mathrm{Prev}^{\mathcal{I}}
.
Thus, by the 6th item of Definition
5
we infer that tiles
\mathrm{t}_{i}
and
\mathrm{t}_{j}
are
\mathrm{H}
-compatible.
The proof of the other statement is routine: take
\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{O}}
to be a
\mathrm{T}
-octant representing
\xi
.
W.l.o.g. assume that
\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{O}}
interprets all role names and concept names that are not mentioned in its definitions as empty sets.
We next rename the domain of
\mathcal{I}_{\mathbb{O}}
to
\mathbb{O}\times\{0\}
and append fresh elements to make the domain equal to
\mathbb{O}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{N}{+}1}\cup\{({-}i{-}1,0,0)\mid i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{N}}\}
.
Then, we enlarge the interpretations of
\mathit{r},\mathit{s},\mathrm{In}
, and
\mathrm{Cur}
in a minimal way according to the first five items of Definition
5
.
Finally, we interpret
\mathrm{Prev}
as the set composed of all triples
(n{+}1,m,k)
for all
(n,m)\in\mathbb{O}
carrying a tile
\mathrm{t}_{k}
, and all triples
(n,n,\ell)
for
x\in\mathbb{N}
and
\ell
denoting the index of
in
\mathrm{T}
.
Call the resulting interpretation
\mathcal{I}
.
Clearly,
\mathcal{I}
is
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctant due to the fact that
\xi
is a map and respects conditions
(OInit):
,
(OVerti):
, and
(OHori):
.

We employ a
\mathbb{VPL}
-CQ
\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}(\mathit{u}_{1},\mathit{u}_{2},\mathit{v}_{1},\mathit{v}_{2},\mathit{w}_{1},\mathit{w}_{2},\mathit{x}_{1},\mathit{x}_{2},\mathit{y}_{1},\mathit{y}_{2},\mathit{z}_{1},\mathit{z}_{2})
as a tool for detecting whether a given
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctant
\mathcal{I}
is proper.
Observe that
\mathcal{I}

is not
proper if and only if there is a position
(n,m)\in\mathbb{O}
and a number
1\leq k\leq\mathrm{N}
, for which we have
(n,m,k)\in\mathrm{Cur}^{\mathcal{I}}
and
(n{+}1,m,k)\not\in\mathrm{Prev}^{\mathcal{I}}
.
This is precisely the condition that is going to be expressed with
\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
, informally presented at Figure
5
.
The intuition behind
\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
is as follows.

Figure 5.
Visualisation of the query
\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}(\mathit{u}_{1},\mathit{u}_{2},\mathit{v}_{1},\mathit{v}_{2},\mathit{w}_{1},\mathit{w}_{2},\mathit{x}_{1},\mathit{x}_{2},\mathit{y}_{1},\mathit{y}_{2},\mathit{z}_{1},\mathit{z}_{2})
.

We first ensure that
\mathit{z}_{1},\mathit{z}_{2}
are mapped to some elements representing the coordinates
(n,m)
and
(n^{\prime},m^{\prime})
of the octant for some integers satisfying
n^{\prime}=n{+}1
and
m=m^{\prime}
.
The fact that they belong to the octant is handled by means of the
\mathrm{In}
concept.
The equality
n^{\prime}=n{+}1
is achieved by introducing variables
\mathit{y}_{1},\mathit{y}_{2}
, stating their
\mathit{r}
-connectedness, and the
\mathit{s}^{*}
-reachability of
\mathit{z}_{1}
and
\mathit{z}_{2}
from, respectively,
\mathit{y}_{1}
and
\mathit{y}_{2}
.
Thus
\mathit{y}_{1},\mathit{y}_{2}
are placed “at the bottom” of the variables
\mathit{z}_{1}
and
\mathit{z}_{2}
.
Next, the equi-hight of
\mathit{z}_{1},\mathit{z}_{2}
is ensured with extra
\mathit{r}
-connected variables
\mathit{x}_{1},\mathit{x}_{2}
located to the left of
\mathit{y}_{1}
, and non-regular atoms
{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}(\mathit{x}_{1},\mathit{z}_{1})
and
{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}(\mathit{x}_{2},\mathit{z}_{2})
, enforcing equality of the distance between
\mathit{x}_{i}
and
\mathit{y}_{i}
, and the distance between
\mathit{y}_{i}
and
\mathit{z}_{i}
, for all
i\in\{1,2\}
.
Once we know that the variables
\mathit{z}_{1},\mathit{z}_{2}
are mapped by a query as desired, we need to express that they violate properness of
\mathcal{I}
.
Recall that we want to establish
(n,m,k)\in\mathrm{Cur}^{\mathcal{I}}
and
(n{+}1,m,k)\not\in\mathrm{Prev}^{\mathcal{I}}
for some
k
.
Such elements will be represented, respectively, by variables
\mathit{v}_{1}
and
\mathit{v}_{2}
.
To express the mentioned constraint, we introduce fresh
\mathit{r}
-connected variables
\mathit{u}_{1},\mathit{u}_{2}
that are located to the left of
\mathit{x}_{1},\mathit{x}_{2}
, and whose distance to
\mathit{u}_{1},\mathit{u}_{2}
will be precisely the
k
that we are looking for.
We stress that we do not “hardcode” the value of
k
in the query.
As the variable
\mathit{u}_{1}
is free to map whenever it wants, this mimics a disjunction over possible values of
k
.
We ensure the variables
\mathit{v}_{1},\mathit{v}_{2}
are mapped to elements outside the octant by expressing that they are
\mathit{r}^{*}
-reachable from the
\mathit{s}
-successors
\mathit{w}_{1},\mathit{w}_{2}
of
\mathit{z}_{1},\mathit{z}_{2}
, that are labelled with
\overline{\mathrm{In}}
.
Note that just expressing that
\mathit{v}_{1},\mathit{v}_{2}
belong to
\overline{\mathrm{In}}
does not suffice, as the path leading from some of
\mathit{z}_{i}
to
\mathit{v}_{i}
could contain elements in
\mathrm{In}
(which we implicitly forbid).
With non-regular atoms
{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}(\mathit{u}_{1},\mathit{v}_{1})
and
{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}(\mathit{u}_{2},\mathit{v}_{2})
we make sure that the distance between
\mathit{z}_{1}
and
\mathit{v}_{1}
(respectively
\mathit{z}_{2}
and
\mathit{v}_{2}
) is indeed
k
.

Despite the high technicality of our construction, we hope that after reading the above intuition and glancing at Figure
5
, the following definition of the query
\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
should be now understandable:

\displaystyle\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}\coloneqq\mathit{r}(\mathit{u}_{1},\mathit{u}_{2})\land\mathit{r}^{*}(\mathit{u}_{2},\mathit{x}_{1})\land\mathit{r}(\mathit{x}_{1},\mathit{x}_{2})\land\mathit{r}^{*}(\mathit{x}_{2},\mathit{y}_{1})\land\mathit{r}(\mathit{y}_{1},\mathit{y}_{2})\land\mathrm{Cur}(\mathit{v}_{1})\land\overline{\mathrm{Prev}}(\mathit{v}_{2})\land

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{i=1}^{2}\big[\mathit{s}^{*}(\mathit{y}_{i},\mathit{z}_{i})\land\mathrm{In}(\mathit{z}_{i})\land\mathit{s}(\mathit{z}_{i},\mathit{w}_{i})\land\overline{\mathrm{In}}(\mathit{w}_{i})\land\mathit{s}^{*}(\mathit{w}_{i},\mathit{v}_{i})\land{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}(\mathit{x}_{i},\mathit{z}_{i})\land{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}(\mathit{u}_{i},\mathit{v}_{i})\big].

By routine case analysis with a bit of calculations, we can show that:

Lemma 21
.

Let
\mathdutchcal{D}\coloneqq(\mathrm{Col},\mathrm{T},\hbox to7.07pt{\vbox to7.07pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-67.59889pt\lower-60.43852pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 93.81 -83.35 h 9.22 v 9.22 h -9.22 Z}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{71.1319pt}{-56.90552pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{62}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}})
be a domino tilling system and let
\mathcal{I}
be a
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctant.
Then we have that
\mathcal{I}
is proper if and only if
\mathcal{I}\not\models\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
.

Proof 5.4
.

We start from the “only if” direction.
Suppose that
\mathcal{I}
is proper, but there is a match
\eta
witnessing
\mathcal{I}\models\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
.
We provide a few tedious calculations.
As the atoms
\mathit{r}(\mathit{x}_{1},\mathit{x}_{2})
,
\mathit{r}(\mathit{y}_{1},\mathit{y}_{2})
, and
\mathit{r}(\mathit{u}_{1},\mathit{u}_{2})
belong to
\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
, there are
a,b,n\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
\eta(\mathit{u}_{1})=(a,0,0)
,
\eta(\mathit{u}_{2})=(a{+}1,0,0)
,
\eta(\mathit{x}_{1})=(b,0,0)
,
\eta(\mathit{x}_{2})=(b{+}1,0,0)
,
\eta(\mathit{y}_{1})=(n,0,0)
, and
\eta(\mathit{y}_{2})=(n{+}1,0,0)
.
By the presence of atoms
\mathit{r}^{*}(\mathit{u}_{2},\mathit{x}_{1})
and
\mathit{r}^{*}(\mathit{x}_{2},\mathit{y}_{1})
in the query, we infer that
a<b<n
.
From atoms
\mathit{s}^{*}(\mathit{y}_{i},\mathit{z}_{i}),\mathrm{In}(\mathit{z}_{i})
present in
\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
, we can deduce that
\eta(\mathit{z}_{1})=(n,m,0)
,
\eta(\mathit{z}_{2})=(n{+}1,m^{\prime},0)
hold for some
m,m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
.
Next, by the satisfaction of the atoms
{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}(\mathit{x}_{1},\mathit{z}_{1})
and
{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}(\mathit{x}_{2},\mathit{z}_{2})
the equations
m=n-b
and
m^{\prime}=(n{+}1)-(b{+}1)
follow.
Thus
m^{\prime}=m
.
As the next step, we deal with the variables
\mathit{w}_{1}
and
\mathit{w}_{2}
.
From the atoms
\mathit{s}(\mathit{z}_{i},\mathit{w}_{i})
and
\overline{\mathrm{In}}(\mathit{w}_{i})
we can deduce that
\eta(\mathit{w}_{1})=(n,m,1)
,
\eta(\mathit{w}_{2})=(n{+}1,m,1)
.
Together with atoms
\mathit{s}^{*}(\mathit{w}_{1},\mathit{v}_{1})
and
\mathit{s}^{*}(\mathit{w}_{1},\mathit{v}_{1})
, this implies that
\eta(\mathit{v}_{1})=(n,m,k)
, and
\eta(\mathit{z}_{2})=(n{+}1,m,k^{\prime})
hold for some positive
k,k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
.
By the satisfaction of the atoms
{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}(\mathit{u}_{1},\mathit{v}_{1})
and
{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}(\mathit{u}_{2},\mathit{v}_{2})
the equations
m{+}k=n{-}a
and
m{+}k^{\prime}=(n{+}1)-(a{+}1)
follow.
Hence,
k=k^{\prime}
holds and by collecting all the previous equations we conclude that
\eta(\mathit{v}_{1})=(n,m,k)
and
\eta(\mathit{v}_{1})=(n{+}1,m,k)
.
Finally, the atoms
\mathrm{Cur}(\mathit{v}_{1})
and
\overline{\mathrm{Prev}}(\mathit{v}_{2})
of
\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
imply that
(n,m,k)\in\mathrm{Cur}^{\mathcal{I}}
but
(n{+}1,m,k)\not\in\mathrm{Prev}^{\mathcal{I}}
.
This contradicts the properness of
\mathcal{I}
.

We show the “if” direction by contraposition.
Suppose that
\mathcal{I}
is not proper, and that the properness of
\mathcal{I}
is violated by
(n,m,k)\in\mathrm{Cur}^{\mathcal{I}}
and
(n{+}1,m,k)\in\overline{\mathrm{Prev}}^{\mathcal{I}}
.
Then the map
\mathit{v}_{1}\mapsto(n,m,k)
,
\mathit{v}_{2}\mapsto(n{+}1,m,k)
,
\mathit{w}_{1}\mapsto(n,m,1)
,
\mathit{w}_{2}\mapsto(n{+}1,m,1)
,
\mathit{z}_{1}\mapsto(n,m,0)
,
\mathit{z}_{2}\mapsto(n{+}1,m,0)
,
\mathit{y}_{1}\mapsto(n,0,0)
,
\mathit{y}_{2}\mapsto(n{+}1,0,0)
,
\mathit{x}_{1}\mapsto(n{-}m,0,0)
,
\mathit{x}_{2}\mapsto(n{-}m{+}1,0,0)
,
\mathit{u}_{1}\mapsto(n{-}m{-}k,0,0)
,
\mathit{u}_{2}\mapsto(n{-}m{-}k{+}1,0,0)
is a match for
\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
. This finishes the proof.

As a side remark, note that if the value of
n{-}m
is sufficiently small, the value of
n{-}m{-}k
can be negative (but not smaller than
-\mathrm{N}
). This explains at last why we appended an incoming
\mathit{r}^{*}
-path of length
\mathrm{N}
to the element
(0,0,0)
in the construction of hyperoctants.

As the final step of our construction, we define an
\mathcal{ALC}
-TBox
\mathcal{T}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
whose intended tree-like models will contain
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctants.
Most of the axioms written below are formalizations of straightforward properties satisfied by
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctants and grids.
As we are aiming to design an
\mathcal{ALC}
-TBox, all the properties expressed in
\mathcal{T}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
are interpreted “globally”. Hence, to express existence of a starting point of a
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctant we employ a fresh role name
\mathit{aux}
and say that every element has an
\mathit{aux}
-successor (with the intended meaning that such a successors “starts” a
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctant).
For brevity, we say that an element is inner (resp. outer) if it belongs (resp. does not belong) to the interpretation of
\mathrm{In}
.
For a language
\mathdutchbcal{L}
we also say that an element
\mathrm{e}
in
\mathcal{I}
is
\mathdutchbcal{L}
-outer-reachable from
\mathrm{d}
if there exists a
\mathdutchbcal{L}
-path from
\mathrm{d}
to
\mathrm{e}
composed solely of outer elements (with a possible exception of
\mathrm{d}
).

(GStart):

Every element has an
\mathit{aux}
-successor.
Every such successor has an outgoing
\mathit{r}^{\mathrm{N}}
-path composed of outer elements that leads to an inner element carrying a tile
that has an inner
\mathit{r}
-successor carrying a tile different from
.

(GCompl):

Concept name
\overline{\mathrm{In}}
(resp.
\overline{\mathrm{Prev}}
) is interpreted as the complement of the interpretation of concept name
\mathrm{In}
(resp.
\mathrm{Prev}
).

(GTil):

Every inner element is labelled with precisely one concept name from
\mathrm{C}_{\text{\faIcon{puzzle-piece}}}^{\mathrm{T}}
, and there are no outer elements labelled with such concept names.

(GSuc):

If an element has an inner
\mathit{r}
-successor then such a successor also has an inner
\mathit{r}
-successor.
Every inner element has an inner
\mathit{s}
-successor.
4
4

4

This ensures for each
i\in\mathbb{N}
the existence of
i

\mathit{s}
-successors for the
i
-th element in the bottom of the octant.

(GPath):

Every inner element has an outgoing
\mathit{s}^{\mathrm{N}}
-path composed solely of outer elements.

(GCur):

For every inner element carrying the tile
\mathrm{t}_{k}
we have that:
(i) all
\mathit{s}^{k}
-outer-reachable elements are labelled with
\mathrm{Cur}
,
(ii) there is no
\mathit{s}^{\ell}
-outer-reachable element, for
\ell\leq\mathrm{N}
and
\ell\neq k
, that is labelled with
\mathrm{Cur}
.

(GPrev):

For every inner element there exists a number
k\leq\mathrm{N}
such that:
(i) all
\mathit{s}^{k}
-outer-reachable elements are labelled with
\mathrm{Prev}
,
(ii) there is no
\mathit{s}^{\ell}
-outer-reachable element, for
\ell\leq\mathrm{N}
and
\ell\neq k
, that is labelled with
\mathrm{Prev}
.

(GVerti):

Every pair of tiles carried by
\mathit{s}
-successors is
\mathrm{V}
-compatible.

(GHori):

For all tiles
\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}
and all elements carrying
\mathrm{t}
that can
\mathit{s}^{\ell}
-outer-reach an element labelled with
\mathrm{Prev}
for some
\ell
, we have that
\mathrm{t}_{\ell}
and
\mathrm{t}
are
\mathrm{H}
-compatible.

The definition of
\mathcal{T}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
is provided in the proof of the following lemma.

Lemma 22
.

There exists an
\mathcal{ALC}
-TBox
\mathcal{T}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
expressing the above properties.

Proof 5.5
.

Given
\mathcal{ALC}
-concepts
\mathrm{C},\mathrm{D}
and a role name
\mathit{r}\in\mathbf{N_{R}}
we employ macros
(\forall{\mathit{r}}.\mathrm{C})^{n}.\mathrm{D}
and
(\exists{\mathit{r}}.\mathrm{C})^{n}.\mathrm{D}
defined inductively for
n\geq 1
as follows:

(\forall{\mathit{r}}.\mathrm{C})^{1}.\mathrm{D}\coloneqq\forall{\mathit{r}}.(\mathrm{C}\to\mathrm{D}),\qquad(\forall{\mathit{r}}.\mathrm{C})^{n{+}1}.\mathrm{D}\coloneqq\forall{\mathit{r}}.(\mathrm{C}\to[(\forall{\mathit{r}}.\mathrm{C})^{n}.\mathrm{D}]),

(\exists{\mathit{r}}.\mathrm{C})^{1}.\mathrm{D}\coloneqq\exists{\mathit{r}}.(\mathrm{C}\sqcap\mathrm{D}),\qquad(\exists{\mathit{r}}.\mathrm{C})^{n{+}1}.\mathrm{D}\coloneqq\exists{\mathit{r}}.(\mathrm{C}\sqcap[(\exists{\mathit{r}}.\mathrm{C})^{n}.\mathrm{D}]).

Our
\mathcal{T}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
is composed of all the GCIs listed below,
describing properties
(GStart):
,
(GCompl):
,
(GTil):
,
(GSuc):
,
(GPath):
,
(GCur):
,
(GPrev):
,
(GVerti):
, and
(GHori):
in precisely this order.

\displaystyle\top

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq(\exists{\mathit{aux}}.\top)\sqcap\forall{\mathit{aux}}.\left((\exists{\mathit{r}}.\overline{\mathrm{In}})^{\mathrm{N}}.[\exists{\mathit{r}}.\left(\mathrm{In}\sqcap\mathrm{C}_{\hbox to7.51pt{\vbox to7.51pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
