\displaystyle\top

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq(\overline{\mathrm{In}}\leftrightarrow\neg\mathrm{In})\sqcap(\overline{\mathrm{Prev}}\leftrightarrow\neg\mathrm{Prev}),

\displaystyle\top

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq[(\bigsqcup_{\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}}\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{t}})\to\mathrm{In}]\sqcap[\mathrm{In}\to(\bigsqcup_{\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}}(\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{t}}\sqcap\mathop{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}}_{\mathrm{t}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{T}\setminus\{\mathrm{t}\}}\neg\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{t}^{\prime}})],

\displaystyle\top

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq(\forall{\mathit{r}}.[\mathrm{In}\to(\exists{\mathit{r}}.\mathrm{In})])\sqcap(\mathrm{In}\to\exists{\mathit{s}}.\mathrm{In}),

\displaystyle\top

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\mathrm{In}\to(\exists{\mathit{s}}.\overline{\mathrm{In}})^{\mathrm{N}}.\top,

\displaystyle\top

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\mathop{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}}_{1\leq k\leq\mathrm{N}}\left(\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{t}_{k}}\to\Big[[(\forall{\mathit{s}}.\overline{\mathrm{In}})^{k}.\mathrm{Cur}]\sqcap\mathop{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}}_{1\leq\ell\leq\mathrm{N},\ \ell\neq k}[(\forall{\mathit{s}}.\overline{\mathrm{In}})^{\ell}.\neg\mathrm{Cur}]\Big]\right),

\displaystyle\top

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\bigsqcup_{1\leq k\leq\mathrm{N}}\left([(\forall{\mathit{s}}.\overline{\mathrm{In}})^{k}.\mathrm{Prev}]\sqcap\mathop{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}}_{1\leq\ell\leq\mathrm{N},\ \ell\neq k}[(\forall{\mathit{s}}.\overline{\mathrm{In}})^{\ell}.\neg\mathrm{Prev}]\right),

\displaystyle\top

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\bigsqcup_{\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}}\left(\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{t}}\to[\mathop{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}}_{\mathrm{t}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{T},(\mathrm{t},\mathrm{t}^{\prime})\ \text{are not}\ \mathrm{V}\text{-compatible}}\neg\exists{\mathit{s}}.\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{t}^{\prime}}]\right),

\displaystyle\top

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq\bigsqcup_{\mathrm{t}\in\mathrm{T}}\left(\mathrm{C}_{\mathrm{t}}\to[\mathop{\mathchoice{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\displaystyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\textstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\scriptscriptstyle\bigsqcup$}}}_{\mathrm{t}_{\ell}\in\mathrm{T},(\mathrm{t}_{\ell},\mathrm{t})\ \text{are not}\ \mathrm{H}\text{-compatible}}\neg(\exists{\mathit{s}}.\overline{\mathrm{In}})^{\ell}.\mathrm{Prev}]\right).

It should be clear that the above GCIs formalise the required properties.

We first see that
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctant can be extended to (counter)models of
\mathcal{T}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
and
\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
.

Lemma 23
.

Every proper
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctant can be extended to a model of
\mathcal{T}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
that violates
\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
.

Proof 5.6
.

Let
\mathcal{I}
be a proper
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctant.
Consider an interpretation
\mathcal{J}
with the domain
\Delta^{\mathcal{J}}\coloneqq\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\cup((\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N})\setminus\mathbb{O})\times\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{N}{+}1}
defined as follows:

•

\mathcal{J}
restricted to
\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{I}
,

•

\mathit{s}^{\mathcal{J}}\coloneqq\mathit{s}^{\mathcal{I}}\cup\{((n,m,k),(n,m,k{+}1)),((n,m,0),(n,m{+}1,0))\mid(n,m)\in(\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N})\setminus\mathbb{O},k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\mathrm{N}}\}
,

•

\mathit{r}^{\mathcal{J}}\coloneqq\mathit{r}^{\mathcal{I}}
,

•

\mathit{aux}^{\mathcal{J}}\coloneqq\Delta^{\mathcal{J}}\times\{(-\mathrm{N},0,0)\}
, and

•

For each concept name
\mathrm{A}
, each tuple
(n,m,k)
with
(n,m)\not\in\mathbb{O}
belongs
\mathrm{A}^{\mathcal{J}}
iff
(0,0,k)\in\mathrm{A}^{\mathcal{I}}
.

Intuitively, we enlarged
\mathcal{I}
to a grid and filled fresh places with copies of the diagonal of
\mathcal{I}
(this is needed to fulfil the second conjunct of the axiomatisation of
(GSuc):
).
By construction of
\mathcal{J}
(and the fact that
\mathcal{I}
is a
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctant) it follows
\mathcal{J}
is a model of
\mathcal{T}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
.
Call
\mathcal{J}

proper
if for all
(n,m,k)\in\mathrm{Cur}^{\mathcal{J}}
we have
(n{+}1,m,k)\in\mathrm{Prev}^{\mathcal{J}}
.
Note that as
\mathcal{I}
is proper, the above condition holds for all triples
(n,m,k)
with
(n,m)\in\mathbb{O}
.
For other tuples, we simply use the fact that the interpretation of concepts for
(n,m,k)
with
(n,m)\not\in\mathbb{O}
is inherited from the elements of the form
(0,0,k)
.
Thus
\mathcal{J}
is indeed proper. Now, without any further changes, the proof of the “only if” direction of Lemma
21
establishes
\mathcal{J}\not\models\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
.

As a handy lemma used in the forthcoming proof, we need to establish that:

Lemma 24
.

Every single-role tree-like model of
\mathcal{T}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
contains a substructure that is isomorphic to a
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctant.

Proof 5.7
.

The proof idea is simple but tedious.
We take single-role tree-like model
\mathcal{I}
of
\mathcal{T}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
, and select elements that will later constitute a hyperoctant branch-by-branch.
Let
\mathrm{d}_{(-\mathrm{N},0,0)}
be any element of
\mathcal{I}
which is an
\mathit{aux}
-successor of some element of
\mathcal{I}
(it exists as
\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}\neq\emptyset
and
\mathcal{I}
satisfies
(GStart):
).
Thus there exists an
\mathit{r}^{*}
-path of elements
\mathrm{d}_{(-\mathrm{N},0,0)},\mathrm{d}_{(-\mathrm{N}{+}1,0,0)},\ldots,\mathrm{d}_{(0,0,0)},

\mathrm{d}_{(1,0,0)}
witnessing the satisfaction of
(GStart):
, where only
\mathrm{d}_{(0,0,0)},\mathrm{d}_{(1,0,0)}
belong to
\mathrm{In}^{\mathcal{J}}
(by
(GStart):
and
(GCompl):
),
\mathrm{d}_{(0,0,0)}
carries a tile
and
\mathrm{d}_{(1,0,0)}
carries a tile that differs from
.
After employing a routine induction, we see that the first conjunct of
(GSuc):
yields the existence of an infinite
\mathit{r}^{*}
-path
\mathrm{d}_{(1,0,0)},\mathrm{d}_{(2,0,0)},\ldots
of inner elements.
By the second conjunct of
(GSuc):
we know that every
\mathrm{d}_{(i,0,0)}
has an outgoing
\mathit{s}^{i}
-path
\mathrm{d}_{(i,1,0)},\ldots,\mathrm{d}_{(i,i,0)}
composed of inner elements.
Finally, by
(GPath):
, every element
\mathrm{d}_{(i,j,0)}
has an outgoing
\mathit{s}^{\mathrm{N}}
-path
\mathrm{d}_{(i,j,1)}
,
\ldots
,
\mathrm{d}_{(i,j,\mathrm{N})}
composed of outer elements.
Note that by tree-likeness of
\mathcal{I}
we have that all of selected elements
\mathrm{d}_{(i,j,k)}
are pairwise-different, and any pair of elements is connected by at most one role.
Let
\mathcal{J}
be the restriction of
\mathcal{I}
to the set of selected element, with the domain renamed with a map
\mathrm{d}_{(i,j,k)}\mapsto(i,j,k)
.
It follows from
(GTil):
and
(GCompl):
that
\mathcal{J}
restricted to
\mathrm{In}^{\mathcal{J}}
is a
\mathrm{T}
-octant.
Other properties of
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctants can be readily verified with
(GCur):
,
(GPrev):
,
(GVerti):
,
(GHori):
.
Thus
\mathcal{J}
is a
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctant, as desired.

The next lemma summarises our reduction.

Lemma 25
.

\mathdutchcal{D}
covers the octant if and only if
\mathcal{T}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}\not\models\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
.

Proof 5.8
.

Suppose that
\mathdutchcal{D}
covers the octant.
Then, by Lemma
20
, there exists a proper
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctant
\mathcal{I}
.
Hence, from Lemma
23
we conclude existence of a model
\mathcal{J}
of
\mathcal{T}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
such that
\mathcal{J}\not\models\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
.
Thus
\mathcal{T}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}\not\models\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
.
For the other direction, suppose that there exists an interpretation
\mathcal{I}
such that
\mathcal{I}\models\mathcal{T}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
but
\mathcal{I}\not\models\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
.
By Corollary
1
we can assume that
\mathcal{I}
is single-role tree-like.
From Lemma
24
we know that
\mathcal{I}
contains a substructure
\mathcal{J}
that is a
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctant.
As
\mathcal{I}\not\models\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
, we know that
\mathcal{J}\not\models\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
.
Thus
\mathcal{J}
is a proper
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctant.
Hence, by Lemma
20
we conclude that
\mathdutchcal{D}
covers the octant.

As the octant tiling problem is undecidable, we conclude the main theorem of this section.

Theorem 26
.

The entailment problem of
\{\mathit{r},\mathit{s},\mathit{r}^{*},\mathit{s}^{*},{\mathit{r}}^{\#}\hskip-1.99997pt{\mathit{s}}^{\#}\}
-conjunctive-queries over
\mathcal{ALC}
-TBoxes is undecidable.

Interestingly enough
5
5

5

I would like to thank Anni-Yasmin Turhan for asking this question.
, our proof technique can be adjusted (with little effort) to derive related results for query languages involving inverses.
Before moving to the undecidability result, let us define the class of
Visibly-Pushdown Path Queries

[
LL15
, p. 330]
(VPQs) as the class of single-atom
\mathbb{VPL}
-CQs (
i
.
e
.
\mathbb{VPL}
-CQs without conjunction).
We stress that the entailment problem of such queries is decidable.
The main idea is that the non-satisfaction of a VPQ of the form
\mathdutchbcal{L}(\mathit{x},\mathit{y})
can be expressed with an
\mathcal{ALC}_{\textsf{vpl}}
-GCI
\top\sqsubseteq\neg\exists{\mathdutchbcal{L}}.\top
. Indeed:

Corollary 27
.

The entailment problem of VPQs over
\mathcal{ALC}_{\textsf{vpl}}
-TBoxes is
2ExpTime
-complete (assuming that the
\mathbb{VPL}
s from the query and the TBox are over the same alphabet).

Proof 5.9
.

The lower bound is inherited from the concept satisfiability problem for
\mathcal{ALC}_{\textsf{vpl}}

[
LLS07
, Thm. 19]
.
Let
\mathcal{T}
be an
\mathcal{ALC}_{\textsf{vpl}}
-TBox and
\mathit{q}
be a VPQ of the form
\mathdutchbcal{L}(\mathit{x},\mathit{y})
.
The crucial observation is that by the semantics of queries we have that
\mathcal{T}\not\models\mathit{q}
if and only if
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}\coloneqq\mathcal{T}\cup\{\top\sqsubseteq\neg\exists{\mathdutchbcal{L}}.\top\}
has a model.
Hence, by a well-known internalisation of TBoxes as concepts in the presence of regular expressions
[
BCM+03
, p. 186]
, we can compute (in time polynomial w.r.t.
|\mathcal{T}|
) an
\mathcal{ALC}_{\textsf{vpl}}
-concept
\mathrm{C}
that is satisfiable if and only if
\mathcal{T}^{\prime}
is.
Now it suffices to check the satisfiability of
\mathrm{C}
.
This in turn can be done in doubly-exponential time by the result of Löding et al.
[
LLS07
, Thm. 18]
, finishing the proof.

We next extend the class of VPQs with an inverse operator, obtaining the class of
Two-Way Visibly Pushdown Path Queries
(2VPQs). More precisely, such queries are VPQs that allow for letters of the form
\mathit{r}^{-}
for all role names
\mathit{r}\in\mathbf{N_{R}}
in the underlying visibly-pushdown alphabet. When evaluating 2VPQs over interpretations
\mathcal{I}
, the role
(\mathit{r}^{-})^{\mathcal{I}}
is interpreted as the set-theoretic inverse of the role
\mathit{r}^{\mathcal{I}}
.
In what follows we sketch the proof of the fact that the entailment of 2VPQs over
\mathcal{ALC}
-TBoxes is undecidable.
The key ingredients of our reduction are the previously-defined
\mathcal{ALC}
-TBox
\mathcal{T}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
and a fresh 2VPQ
\mathit{q}_{{\textrm{2VPQ}}}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}\coloneqq\mathdutchbcal{L}_{\downarrow\rightarrow\uparrow}(\mathit{x},\mathit{y})
, where

\mathdutchbcal{L}_{\downarrow\rightarrow\uparrow}\!\coloneqq\!\Big\{\mathrm{Cur}?\ \overline{\mathrm{In}}?\left(\mathit{s}^{-}\;\overline{\mathrm{In}}?\right)^{k}\ \mathit{s}\;(\mathrm{In}?\ \mathit{s})^{m}\;(\mathrm{In}?\;\mathit{r}\;\mathrm{In}?)\;(\mathit{s}\ \mathrm{In}?)^{m}\mathit{s}\left(\overline{\mathrm{In}}?\;\mathit{s}\right)^{k}\overline{\mathrm{In}}?\ \overline{\mathrm{Prev}}?\!\mid\!k,m\in\mathbb{N}\Big\}.

It is an easy exercise to construct a pushdown automaton for the language
\mathdutchbcal{L}_{\downarrow\rightarrow\uparrow}
. Such an automaton becomes visibly-pushdown under the requirement that
\mathit{s}
and
\mathit{s}^{-}
are, respectively, return and call symbols, and
\mathit{r}
is an internal symbol.
The forthcoming Lemma
28
relates the queries
\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
and
\mathit{q}_{{\textrm{2VPQ}}}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
.
As it can be established analogously to the other lemmas of this section, we only sketch the proof and leave some minor details to the reader.

Lemma 28
.

Let
\mathdutchcal{D}\coloneqq(\mathrm{Col},\mathrm{T},\hbox to7.07pt{\vbox to7.07pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-67.59889pt\lower-60.43852pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 93.81 -83.35 h 9.22 v 9.22 h -9.22 Z}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{71.1319pt}{-56.90552pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{62}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}})
be a domino tilling system and let
\mathcal{I}
be a
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctant.
We have that
\mathcal{I}\models\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
if and only if
\mathcal{I}\models\mathit{q}_{{\textrm{2VPQ}}}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
.
Moreover, by applying the construction from Lemma
23
, every proper
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctant can be extended to a model of
\mathcal{T}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
that violates
\mathit{q}_{{\textrm{2VPQ}}}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
.

Proof 5.10

(Proof sketch.)
.

We first sketch the proof of the equivalence
\mathcal{I}\models\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
if and only if
\mathcal{I}\models\mathit{q}_{{\textrm{2VPQ}}}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
.
First, take any match
\eta
witnessing
\mathcal{I}\models\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
.
Then it can be readily verified that the mapping
\mathit{x}\mapsto\eta(\mathit{v}_{1}),\mathit{y}\mapsto\eta(\mathit{v}_{2})
is a match for
\mathcal{I}
and
\mathit{q}_{{\textrm{2VPQ}}}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
.
For the opposite direction, let
\mathrm{d},\mathrm{e}\in\Delta^{\mathcal{I}}
be domain elements for which the mapping
\mathit{x}\mapsto\mathrm{d},\mathit{y}\mapsto\mathrm{e}
is a match for
\mathcal{I}
and
\mathit{q}_{{\textrm{2VPQ}}}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
.
Hence,
\mathrm{e}
is
\textstyle[\overline{\mathrm{In}}?\left(\mathit{s}^{-}\;\overline{\mathrm{In}}?\right)^{k}\ \mathit{s}\;(\mathrm{In}?\ \mathit{s})^{m}\;(\mathrm{In}?\;\mathit{r}\;\mathrm{In}?)\;(\mathit{s}\ \mathrm{In}?)^{m}\mathit{s}\left(\overline{\mathrm{In}}?\;\mathit{s}\right)^{k}\overline{\mathrm{In}}?]
-reachable from
\mathrm{d}
for some integers
m,k\in\mathbb{N}
.
By analysing the shape of
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctants and the above expression, we infer the existence of an integer
n\in\mathbb{N}
for which
\mathrm{d}=(n,m,k)
, and
\mathrm{e}=(n{+}1,m,k)
. Indeed, the equality of the second and third coordinates is ensured by the presence of
m
and
k
in the above path expression; the fact that the first coordinate of
\mathrm{e}
is the successor value of the first coordinate of
\mathrm{d}
is guaranteed by the subexpression
(\mathrm{In}?\;\mathit{r}\;\mathrm{In}?)
in
\mathit{q}_{{\textrm{2VPQ}}}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
.
Now the match for
\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
and
\mathcal{I}
can be defined as:

\mathit{v}_{1}\mapsto(n,m,k)
,
\mathit{v}_{2}\mapsto(n{+}1,m,k)
,
\mathit{w}_{1}\mapsto(n,m,1)
,
\mathit{w}_{2}\mapsto(n{+}1,m,1)
,
\mathit{z}_{1}\mapsto(n,m,0)
,
\mathit{z}_{2}\mapsto(n{+}1,m,0)
,
\mathit{y}_{1}\mapsto(n,0,0)
,
\mathit{y}_{2}\mapsto(n{+}1,0,0)
,
\mathit{x}_{1}\mapsto(n{-}m,0,0)
,
\mathit{x}_{2}\mapsto(n{-}m{+}1,0,0)
,
\mathit{u}_{1}\mapsto(n{-}m{-}k,0,0)
,
\mathit{u}_{2}\mapsto(n{-}m{-}k{+}1,0,0)
.

For the remaining part of the proof, take any proper
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctant
\mathcal{I}
.
We apply the construction from the proof of Lemma
23
, and obtain a model
\mathcal{J}
of
\mathcal{T}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
that violates
\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
.
It suffices to show that
\mathcal{J}\not\models\mathit{q}_{{\textrm{2VPQ}}}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
. Towards a contradiction, suppose the opposite, and take any match
\eta
for
\mathcal{J}
and
\mathit{q}_{{\textrm{2VPQ}}}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
.
Then, by the construction of
\mathcal{J}
and the shape of
\mathit{q}_{{\textrm{2VPQ}}}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
, we conclude that the image of
\eta
belongs to the
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctant part
\mathcal{I}
of
\mathcal{J}
.
By equivalence between the queries
\mathit{q}_{{\textrm{2VPQ}}}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
and
\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
sketched above, we conclude
\mathcal{I}\models\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
. A contradiction.

The second auxiliary lemma has a proof analogous to the proof Lemma
25
.

Lemma 29
.

Let
\mathdutchcal{D}\coloneqq(\mathrm{Col},\mathrm{T},\hbox to7.07pt{\vbox to7.07pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip-67.59889pt\lower-60.43852pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 93.81 -83.35 h 9.22 v 9.22 h -9.22 Z}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{71.1319pt}{-56.90552pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{62}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}})
be a domino tilling system and let
\mathcal{I}
be a
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctant.
We have that
\mathcal{T}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}\not\models\mathit{q}_{{\textrm{2VPQ}}}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
if and only if
\mathdutchcal{D}
covers the octant.

Proof 5.11
.

If
\mathdutchcal{D}
covers the octant, by Lemma
20
there exists a proper
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctant
\mathcal{I}
.
Applying the second part of Lemma
28
, we extend
\mathcal{I}
to a model of
\mathcal{T}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
violating
\mathcal{J}\not\models\mathit{q}_{{\textrm{2VPQ}}}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
.
Thus
\mathcal{T}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}\not\models\mathit{q}_{{\textrm{2VPQ}}}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
.
For the reverse direction, take any model
\mathcal{I}
of
\mathcal{T}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
such that
\mathcal{I}\not\models\mathit{q}_{{\textrm{2VPQ}}}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
.
By Corollary
1
we can assume that
\mathcal{I}
is single-role tree-like.
From Lemma
24
we know that
\mathcal{I}
contains a substructure
\mathcal{J}
that is a
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctant.
As
\mathcal{I}\not\models\mathit{q}_{{\textrm{2VPQ}}}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
, we know that
\mathcal{J}\not\models\mathit{q}_{{\textrm{2VPQ}}}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
, and thus, by Lemma
28
, we know that
\mathcal{J}\not\models\mathit{q}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
.
Hence, by Lemma
21
,
\mathcal{J}
is a proper
\mathrm{T}
-hyperoctant.
Invoking Lemma
20
we conclude that
\mathdutchcal{D}
covers the octant.

Linking Lemma
29
with the undecidability of the tiling problem (Lemma
19
), we get:

Theorem 30
.

The entailment problem of 2VPQs over
\mathcal{ALC}
-TBoxes is undecidable.

Once again, let us point out that by the semantics of the query and the logics observe that
\mathcal{T}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}\not\models\mathit{q}_{{\textrm{2VPQ}}}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}
holds if and only if
\mathcal{T}_{\blacktriangle}^{\mathdutchcal{D}}\cup\{\top\sqsubseteq\neg\exists{\mathdutchbcal{L}_{\downarrow\rightarrow\uparrow}}.\top\}
(which is written in
\mathcal{ALC}_{\textsf{vpl}}\mathcal{I}
, namely the extension of
\mathcal{ALC}_{\textsf{vpl}}
with the inverse operator) is satisfiable.
Such a reduction yields undecidability of the concept satisfiability problem for
\mathcal{ALC}_{\textsf{vpl}}\mathcal{I}
, and thus reproves the previously-established result of Göller
[
Göl08
, Prop. 2.32]
(Corollary
2
in Preliminaries).

We conclude the section by revisiting known results concerning query entailment in (extensions of)
\mathcal{ALC}
, and lifting them to the case of
\mathcal{ALC}_{\textsf{vpl}}
.
This contrasts with Theorem
26
.

Corollary 31
.

The query entailment problem for the class of Positive Conjunctive Regular Path Queries over
\mathcal{ALC}_{\textsf{vpl}}
-TBoxes is
2ExpTime
-complete.

Proof 5.12

(Proof sketch.)
.

Note that all the results given above transfer immediately to the case of TBoxes, as they can be internalised in concepts in the presence of regular expressions
[
BCM+03
, p. 186]
. Hence, it suffices to focus on
\mathcal{ALC}_{\textsf{vpl}}
-concepts only.
Let
\mathrm{C}
be an input
\mathcal{ALC}_{\textsf{vpl}}
-concept and
\mathit{q}
be a
\mathbb{REG}
-PEQ.
Löding et al. introduced
[
LLS07
, p. 55]
a model of visibly pushdown tree automata that has the following properties (unfortunately all of them are only implicit in the paper):
(a) generalises nondeterministic tree automata,
(b) is closed under intersection (and an automaton recognizing the intersection of languages can be computed in polynomial time),
and (c) its non-emptiness can be tested in exponential time
[
LLS07
, Thm. 4]
.
They also provided
[
LLS07
, Sec. 4.2]
an automaton
\mathdutchbcal{A}_{\mathrm{C}}
that accepts precisely (suitably) single-role tree-like models of
\mathrm{C}
, and the size of
\mathdutchbcal{A}_{\mathrm{C}}
is exponential w.r.t. the size of the concept
\mathrm{C}

[
LLS07
, Lemma 17]
.
On the other hand, Gutiérrez-Basulto et al. provide
[
GIJM23
, Lemma 8]
a non-deterministic tree automaton
\mathdutchbcal{A}_{\neg\mathit{q}}
, of size exponential w.r.t. the sizes of
\mathrm{C}
and
\mathit{q}
, that accepts all single-role tree-like structures that do not contain any matches of
\mathit{q}
.
6
6

6

In the paper of Gutiérrez-Basulto et al., the query automaton is presented in a more general setting of tree decomposition as it was designed to also work for the case of knowledge-bases (not just TBoxes). In our paper we deal with concepts only, so such an automaton works on trees as usual.

It follows then that the intersection of the languages of
\mathdutchbcal{A}_{\mathrm{C}}
and
\mathdutchbcal{A}_{\neg\mathit{q}}
is non-empty if and only if
\mathrm{C}\not\models\mathit{q}
.
As visibly pushdown tree automata are closed under intersection
[
LLS07
, p. 55]
, and their non-emptiness can be solved in exponential time
[
LLS07
, Thm. 4]
, we infer that the non-emptiness of
\mathdutchbcal{A}_{\mathrm{C}}\cap\mathdutchbcal{A}_{\neg\mathit{q}}
can be tested in doubly-exponential time w.r.t. the sizes of
\mathrm{C}
and
\mathit{q}
.
The matching lower bound is inherited from the concept satisfiability.

6.
Conclusions

We investigated the decidability status of extensions of
\mathcal{ALC}_{\textsf{vpl}}
(also known as Propositional Dynamic Logic with Visibly Pushdown Programs) with popular features supported by W3C ontology languages.
While undecidability of
\mathcal{ALC}_{\textsf{vpl}}
with inverses or role-hierarchies follows from existing work (see the end of Section
2
),
we provided undecidability proofs of
\mathcal{ALC}_{\textsf{vpl}}
extended with the
\mathsf{Self}
operator (Section
3
), with nominals (Section
4
), or non-regular queries (Section
5
).
The first proof relied on the reduction from non-emptiness of the intersection of deterministic one-counter languages. The other two proofs relied on reduction from (variants of) the tiling problem.
We conclude the paper with a list of open problems.

•

Our undecidability proof for
\mathcal{ALC}^{\textsf{Self}}_{\mathsf{vpl}}
relied on the availability of multiple visibly-pushdown languages that are encodings on deterministic one-counter languages. Can our undecidability proof be sharpened?
For instance, is the concept satisfiability of
\mathcal{ALC}_{\textsf{reg}}^{\mathit{r}\#\mathit{s}\#}
with
\mathsf{Self}
already undecidable?

•

Positive results for
\mathcal{ALC}_{\textsf{vpl}}

[
LLS07
, Thm. 18]
concern the concept satisfiability problem, rather than the knowledge-base satisfiability problem.
Is the later decidable for
\mathcal{ALC}_{\textsf{vpl}}
? Classical techniques
[
DL94
, p. 210]
for incorporating ABoxes inside concepts do not work, as the class of visibly-pushdown languages is not compositional (is of “infinite memory”).
Note that this problem already occurs for
\mathcal{ALC}_{\textsf{reg}}^{\mathit{r}\#\mathit{s}\#}
.

•

Is an extension of
\mathcal{ALC}_{\textsf{vpl}}
(or even
\mathcal{ALC}_{\textsf{reg}}^{\mathit{r}\#\mathit{s}\#}
) with functionality decidable?
De Giacomo and Lenzerini
[
DL94
, p. 211]
proposed a satisfiability-preserving translation from
\mathcal{ALC}_{\textsf{reg}}
with functionality to plain
\mathcal{ALC}_{\textsf{reg}}
.
Unfortunately, this reduction does not seem to be applicable to
\mathcal{ALC}_{\textsf{vpl}}
. The reason is again that visibly-pushdown languages are not compositional.
What is more, functionality violates a crucial condition of “unique diamond-path property” from the decidability proof of
\mathcal{ALC}_{\textsf{vpl}}

[
LLS07
, Def. 11]
.

•

Existing positive results on non-regular extensions of
\mathcal{ALC}_{\textsf{reg}}
, especially these of Löding et al.
[
LLS07
, Thm. 18]
, rely on the use of (potentially infinite) tree-like models. Is the
finite
satisfiability problem for
\mathcal{ALC}_{\textsf{vpl}}
decidable? Already the case of
\mathcal{ALC}_{\textsf{reg}}^{\mathit{r}\#\mathit{s}\#}
is open.
